I read every file in scope in full, traced the trust primitives (`governor/authn.py`, `governor/identity.py`, `reuse/crypto.py`, `spine/store.py`), and grepped every consumer of `verify_approval` / `pending` / `authorized_devices`. Verdict: **the cryptographic keystone is SOUND** — but I am raising **2 BLOCK-level honesty/substance findings** plus lower notes. A stream cannot pass RED-PEN while its prose overclaims what the deterministic layer enforces.\n\n## Verdict: BLOCK (honesty/substance) — keystone security property HELD\n\n---\n\n## BLOCK-1 (Honesty — MEDIUM): a device \"approval\" reaches NO execution gate; the docs say it approves A2/A3 items\n\n**Files:** `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/mesh/registry.py:5-9` and `:76-79`; `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/bridge/daemon.py:1-8,46-64`. Enforcement diverges at `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/perception/egress.py:39` and `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/operator.py:234`.\n\n`verify_approval` is called in exactly four places. The two that actually **authorize an effect** call it WITHOUT `extra_pubkeys`, i.e. owner-key-only:\n\n- `perception/egress.py:39` — `... and verify_approval(r, trusted_pubkey)` gates the frontier-vision **upload** (A2 egress off the box).\n- `agents/operator.py:234` — `... and verify_approval(r, tp)` gates **operator plan execution** (file mutation, A2/A3).\n\nOnly `bridge/daemon.py:43` passes `extra_pubkeys=self._authorized()`, and that flows solely into `approvals.pending()` — a **queue view**, consumed by nothing that executes. (`dashboard.py:44` and `ApprovalQueue._target` at `approvals.py:85` also call `pending()` without `extra_pubkeys`.)\n\n**Repro (ATTACK B, run):** an owner-authorized device signs an approval of a queued A2; the owner-only gate that egress/operator use returns `False` for it — the device approval is not counted:\n```\nowner-only gate counts device approval? False\n```\nAnd the phone/owner views diverge (ATTACK D): after a device resolves an A3 item, `pending(...,extra_pubkeys=...)` = `[]` (phone) but `pending(...)` = `[0]` (owner dashboard).\n\n**Why this blocks:** `registry.py:6-9` states \"The phone then approves A2/A3 items by signing with ITS key; `verify_approval(..., extra_pubkeys=authorized)` accepts it,\" and the daemon docstring frames a device-signed approval as the approval of the gated action. In enforced behaviour a device approval **cannot cause any A2/A3 action to execute** — not an egress upload, not an operator plan (the exact high-value queued items a remote phone would exist to approve). The mechanism only clears the phone's own cosmetic queue. This is fail-*safe* (a device can never authorize a dangerous execution — that part is good), but it is an overclaim of enforced capability, which is a hard honesty finding for this review.\n\n**Fix hint:** either (a) correct the registry/daemon docstrings and the program headline to state the true scope (\"a device resolves the phone-side queue view; execution authorization remains owner-key-only\"), or (b) if device-authorization of egress/operator is actually intended, thread `extra_pubkeys=authorized_devices(...)` into those two gates — but that is a real trust expansion of the execution path and must come back through review on its own.\n\n---\n\n## BLOCK-2 (Substance/Honesty — MEDIUM): the bridge transport + bind enforcement are documented as present but not shipped\n\n**Files:** `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/bridge/daemon.py:6-8` (docstring) and `bind_ok` at `:17-26`; `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/cli.py` (diff adds only `sigil host caps`).\n\nThe daemon docstring asserts, present tense: \"the HTTP transport reuses the WS-C server pattern bound to a `bind_ok` address.\" No such server exists. Grep for any binder of `BridgeDaemon`/`PushNotifier`/`bind_ok` returns nothing outside the bridge package itself; the CLI diff adds only `cmd_host`. Consequences:\n\n- `bind_ok` is **dead code** relative to any real listener — the WireGuard/loopback bind doctrine is stated but enforced nowhere in shipped code.\n- `BridgeDaemon.submit_device_approval / panic_engage / relay` and `PushNotifier.poll` are library-only; **no test drives an actual bound socket.**\n\nMitigating: because nothing is bound, there is currently **no exposed attack surface** (this is why CLAIM 3's bind edge cases, while I probed them, aren't independently exploitable). Still, per the honesty rule, \"reuses the WS-C server pattern bound to a bind_ok address\" describes an artifact that doesn't exist in this stream.\n\n**Fix hint:** scope the docstrings to \"library core; the WireGuard HTTP transport is a later slice\" — or ship the `bind_ok`-guarded server with a test that asserts a bind to `0.0.0.0`/public is refused at the socket layer.\n\n---\n\n## Lower-severity notes (not independently blocking; fix alongside)\n\n- **N1 (Test substance — LOW).** The D2 claim \"secrets NEVER enter the spine\" is asserted by **no test** — `test_secret_store_roundtrips_and_env_fallback` only checks a round-trip. It holds by construction (`platform/secrets.py` imports no `SpineStore`), but the property is uncovered. Also the **keyring branch** of `SecretStore.set/get` is never exercised here (`keyring` is absent → envfile path only), and when keyring *is* present that test writes `hunter2` into the real `sigil` service without cleanup (keyring pollution). `tests/test_mobile.py:58-64`.\n\n- **N2 (Test substance — LOW).** `test_device_approval_target_seq_binding_no_replay` (`test_mobile.py:103-115`) exercises only the *weak* replay (mutating `supersedes_id`, leaving the signed `target_seq` = harmless). The stronger vector (mutating the payload's `target_seq` → dangerous, keeping the harmless signature) is not in the suite. I confirmed it empirically (ATTACK A): the item stays pending because `_approval_message` binds `target_seq` into the signature. Property holds; the test is narrower than its assertion string implies.\n\n- **N3 (Footgun — LOW).** `revoke_device` and `authorized_devices` key state on **`device_pubkey`** (`registry.py:57-73`), not `device_id`. Revocation requires the exact pubkey; a revoke with a mismatched/typo'd pubkey silently no-ops and the device stays authorized. Owner-operational hazard (revoke is owner-signed, so not an attacker path), but there is no CLI to enumerate/revoke devices, making the footgun likelier.\n\n- **N4 (Hardening — LOW).** `SecretStore._env_upsert` (`platform/secrets.py:53-73`) `write_text`s the env file under the default umask (often 0644) **then** `chmod 0600` — a brief world-readable window on first creation; `SIGIL_HOME` dir perms are not constrained. Local-only, low severity.\n\n---\n\n## Per-property attestation (what I attacked, and why it held)\n\n**Oracle soundness / trust-widening (CLAIM 1) — HELD.** Negative controls (unauthorized device, self-signed authorization, revoked device) re-run green with real Ed25519. I then tried to *fire the widened path with a benign/rogue input*:\n- Device self-authorizes with its own key → `authorized_devices` ignores it (`verify_signed` pins `pubkey==owner`; ATTACK, `test_forged_and_revoked...`). Rejected.\n- Unsigned/forged `device_authorized` → fail-closed. Rejected.\n- Authorized-then-revoked device, incl. via `submit_device_approval` → refused: `signing device is not authorized` (ATTACK C). Rejected.\n- `pubkey` not in the authorized set → `pub not in allowed` → False. Rejected.\n- **Strong replay** (edit payload `target_seq` from harmless→dangerous, keep the signature) → dangerous stays pending; the signed `target_seq` binds via `_approval_message` (ATTACK A). Rejected.\n- Weak replay (mutate `supersedes_id`) → resolution keys on the signed `target_seq`, not `supersedes_id`. Rejected.\n- Old-authorize overriding newer-revoke → `authorized_devices` takes latest-verified-state in seq order; `iter_records` yields append/seq order over an append-only file (verified idempotent). Rejected.\n- Cross-protocol key confusion → device keys are never in the owner-only `allowed` set, so a device can't satisfy kill-release, promotion, egress, or operator gates (`verify_signed`/`verify_approval` pin `pubkey==owner` there). Rejected.\n\n**No silent promotion — HELD.** A claim the owner-only gate refused (a device approval) cannot reach execution: I traced the only two effect-bearing consumers (`egress.py:39`, `operator.py:234`) and confirmed empirically they don't count device approvals (ATTACK B). No LLM/opinion path exists here — the whole layer is Ed25519 verification. (The *reason* it can't promote is BLOCK-1: the widened path is disconnected from execution.)\n\n**No offensive drift — HELD.** `mesh/`, `bridge/`, `platform/` each call `assert_no_offense()` at import; no `framework.*` leaks (verified). Platform backends are observe-only capture returning honest `None`; `relay` routes to `KernelDispatch` as a fixed argv `[bin, \"ask\", text]` (no shell, WARDEN-gated) — no exploitation/evasion/payload/C2/persistence introduced.\n\n**Test substance — MIXED (see N1/N2).** 10/10 pass with genuine keypairs and real negative controls (self-sign, revoke, daemon-refusal). Gaps: no \"not-on-spine\" assertion, keyring branch untested, replay test narrower than claimed.\n\n**Gate/determinism — HELD.** SIGIL has no `make gate`; the review-relevant functions are pure over the log (no wallclock/RNG in the trust math — `authorized_devices` proven idempotent). Spine `ts` stays out of the content digest (documented).\n\n**Honesty — FAILED (BLOCK-1, BLOCK-2).** The deterministic layer enforces *less* than the docstrings and the \"widens the approval trust root\" headline assert: device approvals authorize no execution, and the WireGuard-bound HTTP transport that `bind_ok` supposedly guards is not shipped.\n\n**CLAIM 2 (no secret/subject leak) — HELD.** `PushNotifier.poll` (`notifier.py:20-29`) emits only `{seq,tier,kind}`; it reads a record but never forwards the tailer's `text` (which `SpineRecord.text()` would populate from the subject). `BridgeDaemon.pending` likewise returns only `{seq,tier,kind}`. `SecretStore` never imports the spine. `test_push_carries_no_subject_or_secret` confirms \"SECRET\"/\"subject\" absent from the push.\n\n**CLAIM 3 (bind + platform honesty) — HELD** (with BLOCK-2 caveat that bind_ok is unwired). `bind_ok` probed across 22 inputs: refuses `0.0.0.0`/`::` (unspecified), all public IPv4/IPv6, `::ffff:<public>`, CGNAT `100.64/10`, multicast, hostnames, empty; allows loopback, RFC1918, ULA `fc00/fd00`, link-local, and `::ffff:<private>`. `panic_engage` posts an *unsigned* halt (honored — fail-safe) and the daemon exposes **no** release method; `KillSwitch.is_engaged` only un-halts on an owner-signed release. `macos`/`windows` backends import cleanly on Linux (native imports are lazy/guarded).\n\n**Bottom line:** the trust-widening cryptography is correct and properly pinned to owner-minted keys — I could not break it. Merge is blocked only until the docs match the shipped enforcement (BLOCK-1) and the transport claim is either shipped-with-a-bind-test or scoped down (BLOCK-2).
